# United States

Several terms redirect here. The **United States of America** (**USA**), also known as the **United States** (**U.S.**) or **America**, is a country primarily located in [North America](21139.md). It is a [federal republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_republic) consisting of 50 [states](18618239.md) and a [federal capital district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_districts_and_territories), [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) The 48 [contiguous states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contiguous_states) border [Canada](5042916.md) to the north and [Mexico](3966054.md) to the south, with the [semi-exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-exclave) of [Alaska](624.md) in the northwest and the [archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archipelago) of [Hawaii](13270.md) in the [Pacific Ocean](23070.md). The United States also asserts sovereignty over five [major island territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territories_of_the_United_States) and [various uninhabited islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Minor_Outlying_Islands) in [Oceania](22621.md) and the [Caribbean](18956035.md).[^cite_note-34] It is a [megadiverse country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_country), with the world's [fourth-largest total area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area)[^cite_note-39] and [third-largest population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population), exceeding 341 million.[^cite_note-pop_clock-40]&#x20;

[Paleo-Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleo-Indians) migrated from North Asia to North America at least 15,000 years ago and formed various civilizations. Beginning in 1607, [British colonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_colonization_of_the_Americas) established the [Thirteen Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies). The 18th-century [American Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Enlightenment) spread values of [republicanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicanism) and [liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism_in_the_United_States) there, and clashes with the [British Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Crown) over [taxation without parliamentary representation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_taxation_without_representation), among other denied [rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rights_of_Englishmen), sparked the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution). Independence from Great Britain was formally declared in the [Lee Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Resolution) of July 2, 1776, two days before the [Declaration of Independence](31874.md). Victory in the 1775–1783 [Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War) brought [international recognition of U.S. sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1783\)).&#x20;

The U.S. [expanded westward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_evolution_of_the_United_States) through the purchase, settlement, and conquest of European- and Indigenous-controlled territory. As more states [joined the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union), a [sectional division over slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_states_and_free_states) led 11 Southern states [to declare secession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinance_of_Secession) and form the [Confederate States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America), fighting the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\)) in the [Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) of 1861–1865. With the United States' victory and reunification, slavery [was abolished](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_slavery_in_the_United_States). By 1900, the U.S. emerged as a [great power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_power), a status solidified after [its involvement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_in_World_War_I) in [World War I](4764461.md). Following [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan)'s [attack on Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor) in 1941, it [entered World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_entry_into_World_War_II) on the [Allied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) side. [The war's aftermath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftermath_of_World_War_II) left the U.S. and the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) as rival [superpowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower), vying for [geopolitical dominance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geopolitical_dominance) during the [Cold War](325329.md). The [Soviet Union's collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) in 1991 left the U.S. as the world's [sole superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sole_superpower), although it has faced competition from [China](5405.md) in the 2020s.&#x20;

The [U.S. federal government](195149.md) is a [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic) under the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Constitution), which created [a separation of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers_under_the_United_States_Constitution) among [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative), [executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_federal_executive_departments), and [judicial](1636627.md) branches. Executive authority is vested in [a president](24113.md), while [Congress](31756.md) is a [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) national legislature composed of the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) (a [lower house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_house) based on population) and the [Senate](24909346.md) (an [upper house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_house) based on equal representation for each state). The [Supreme Court](31737.md) exercises [judicial review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_review_in_the_United_States), under which it may hold laws or governmental actions unconstitutional. [Federalism grants substantial autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism_in_the_United_States) to the 50 states.&#x20;

A [developed country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country), the [United States ranks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_rankings_of_the_United_States) high in economic competitiveness, innovation, and [higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education_in_the_United_States). [Its economy](32022.md) accounts for [over a quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) of nominal global GDP and has been the world's largest [since about 1890](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_largest_historical_GDP). The U.S. is the [wealthiest country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_total_wealth), with the [highest disposable household income per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disposable_household_and_per_capita_income#Household_disposable_income_per_capita_\(OECD\)) among [OECD](33853117.md) members, though [its wealth inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wealth_inequality_in_the_United_States) is highly pronounced. [American culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_United_States), shaped by [centuries of immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_immigration_to_the_United_States), is diverse and [globally influential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americanization). The U.S. makes up [nearly a third](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_with_highest_military_expenditures) of global military spending and is widely considered to have [the most powerful armed forces in the world](32212.md). A member of [numerous international organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_organization_membership_of_the_United_States), it plays a dominant role in global political, cultural, economic, and military affairs.&#x20;

## Etymology

Further information: [Names of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_United_States), [Demonyms for the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demonyms_for_the_United_States), [United Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Colonies), and [Naming of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naming_of_the_Americas)

Documented use of the phrase "United States of America" dates back to January 2, 1776. On that day, [Stephen Moylan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Moylan), a [Continental Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Army) aide to General [George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington), wrote a letter to [Joseph Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Reed_\(politician\)), Washington's [aide-de-camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aide-de-camp), seeking to go "with full and ample powers from the United States of America to Spain" to seek assistance in the [Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War) effort.[^cite_note-DeLear-20132-41][^cite_note-42] The first known public usage is an [anonymous essay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anonymous_work) published in the [Williamsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williamsburg,_Virginia) newspaper *[The Virginia Gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Virginia_Gazette)* on April 6, 1776.[^cite_note-DeLear-20132-41] Sometime on or after June 11, 1776, [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson) wrote "United States of America" in a rough draft of the [Declaration of Independence](31874.md),[^cite_note-DeLear-20132-41] which was adopted by the [Second Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress) on July 4, 1776.[^cite_note-Davis72-43]&#x20;

The term "United States" and its initialism "U.S.", used as nouns or as adjectives in English, are common short names for the country. The initialism "USA", a noun, is also common.[^cite_note-\:1-44] "United States" and "U.S." are the established terms throughout the [U.S. federal government](195149.md), with prescribed rules.[^cite_note-46] "The States" is an established colloquial shortening of the name, used particularly from abroad;[^cite_note-47] "stateside" is the corresponding adjective or adverb.[^cite_note-48]&#x20;

"America" is the feminine form of the first name of *Americus Vesputius*, the Latinized name of Italian explorer [Amerigo Vespucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amerigo_Vespucci) (1454–1512);[^cite_note-49] it was first used as a place name by the German cartographers [Martin Waldseemüller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Waldseem%C3%BCller) and [Matthias Ringmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthias_Ringmann) in 1507.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] Vespucci proposed that the [West Indies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Indies) discovered by [Christopher Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Columbus) in 1492 were part of a previously unknown landmass and not among the Indies at the eastern limit of Asia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESider2007226-52][^cite_note-53][^cite_note-locnamingofamerica-54] In English, [the term "America"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americas_\(terminology\)) (used without a qualifier) seldom refers to topics unrelated to the United States. "The [Americas](29833.md)" is the general term to describe the totality of the continents of [North](21139.md) and [South America](26769.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilson199327–28-55]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States)

For a topical guide, see [Outline of the history of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outline_of_the_history_of_the_United_States).

### Indigenous peoples

Main articles: [History of Native Americans in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) and [Pre-Columbian era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Columbian_era)



[Cliff Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliff_Palace), a settlement of [ancestors of](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestral_Puebloans) the [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) [Pueblo peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_peoples) in present-day [Montezuma County, Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montezuma_County,_Colorado), built between c. 1200 and 1275

[^cite_note-56]

The [first inhabitants of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleo-Indians) migrated from [Siberia](29639.md) at least 15,000 years ago, either across the [Bering land bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_land_bridge) or along the [now-submerged Ice Age coastline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_migration_\(Americas\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEErlandsonRickVellanoweth200819-57][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESavage201155–58-58] Human footprints at [White Sands National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Sands_National_Park) have been dated to about 21,000–23,000 years ago.[^cite_note-Review-WhiteSands2023-59] The [Clovis culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clovis_culture), which appeared around 11,000 BCE in North America, is believed to be the first widespread culture in the Americas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatersStafford20071122–1126-60][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery2015173–185-61] Over time, Indigenous North American cultures grew increasingly sophisticated, and some, such as the [Mississippian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippian_culture), developed [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Agricultural_Complex), [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeastern_Ceremonial_Complex), and [complex societies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_societies).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELockard2010315-62] In the [post-archaic period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Native_Americans_in_the_United_States#Post-Archaic_stage), the Mississippian cultures were located in the [midwestern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States), [eastern](509486.md), and [southern](179553.md) regions, and the [Algonquian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algonquian_peoples) in the [Great Lakes region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes_region) and along the [Eastern Seaboard](89126.md), while the [Hohokam culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hohokam_culture) and [Ancestral Puebloans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestral_Puebloans) inhabited the [Southwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Southwest).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohansen200651-63] [Native population estimates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_history_of_the_Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) of what is now the United States before the arrival of European colonizers range from around 500,000[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThornton199834-64][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerdueGreen200540-65] to nearly 10 million.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerdueGreen200540-65][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHainesHainesSteckel200012-66] In 2024, an estimated 3.43 million people in the United States identified as American Indian or Alaska Native alone (thus, identifying with no other racial category) and represented approximately 1% of the U.S. population.[^cite_note-67] An additional 5.68 million people identified as American Indian or Alaska Native in combination with one or more other races, bringing the total of those identifying as American Indian or Alaska Native to approximately 9.11 million.[^cite_note-68]&#x20;

### European exploration, colonization and conflict (1513–1765)

Main articles: [Colonial history of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_history_of_the_United_States) and [Colonial American military history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_American_military_history)



The [colonial possessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_history_of_the_United_States) of [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_colonization_of_the_Americas) (the Thirteen Colonies in pink and others in purple), [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France) (in blue), and [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Spain) (in orange) in North America, 1750

[Christopher Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Columbus) began exploring the [Caribbean](18956035.md) for Spain in 1492, leading to [Spanish-speaking settlements and missions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) from what are now [Puerto Rico](23041.md) and Florida to [New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico) and [California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alta_California). The first Spanish colony in the present-day continental United States was [Spanish Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Florida), chartered in 1513.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVigil2006329-69][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHudson2018130-70][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis19325–6-71][^cite_note-72] After several settlements failed there due to starvation and disease, Spain's first permanent town, [Saint Augustine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Augustine,_Florida), was founded in 1565.[^cite_note-73]&#x20;

France established its own settlements in [French Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Florida) in 1562, but they were either abandoned (Charlesfort, 1563)[^cite_note-Review-Charlesfort-74] or destroyed by Spanish raids ([Fort Caroline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Caroline), 1565). [Permanent French settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France) were founded much later along the [Great Lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes) ([Fort Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Pontchartrain_du_D%C3%A9troit), 1701), the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River) ([St. Louis](27687.md), 1764) and especially the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md) ([New Orleans](53842.md), 1718).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPetto2007125-75] Early European colonies also included the thriving Dutch colony of [New Nederland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Nederland) (settled 1626, present-day New York) and the small Swedish colony of [New Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Sweden) (settled 1638 in what became Delaware). [British colonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_colonization_of_the_Americas) of the [East Coast](89126.md) began with the [Virginia Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Virginia) (1607) and the [Plymouth Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plymouth_Colony) (Massachusetts, 1620).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESeelyeSelby2018344-76][^cite_note-77]&#x20;

The [Mayflower Compact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayflower_Compact) in Massachusetts and the [Fundamental Orders of Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_Orders_of_Connecticut) established precedents for local representative [self-governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-governance) and [constitutionalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionalism) that would develop throughout the American colonies.[^cite_note-Remini2–32-78][^cite_note-Johnson26–302-79] While European settlers in what is now the United States experienced conflicts with Native Americans, they also engaged in trade, exchanging European tools for food and animal pelts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERipper20086-80][^cite_note-84] Relations ranged from close cooperation to warfare and massacres. The colonial authorities often pursued policies that forced Native Americans to adopt European lifestyles, including conversion to Christianity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERipper20085-85][^cite_note-86] Along the eastern seaboard, settlers [trafficked Africans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_colonial_history_of_the_United_States) through the [Atlantic slave trade](49005.md), largely to provide [manual labor on plantations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_plantation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas1997516-87]&#x20;

The original [Thirteen Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies)[^cite_note-88] that would later found the United States were administered as [possessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dependent_territory) of the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) by Crown-appointed governors,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBilhartzElliott2007-89] though [local governments held elections open to most white male property owners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonial_government_in_the_Thirteen_Colonies).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWood1998263-90][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERatcliffe2013220-91] The colonial population grew rapidly from Maine to Georgia, eclipsing Native American populations;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaltonRockoff200938–39-92] by the 1770s, the natural increase of the population was such that only a small minority of Americans had been born overseas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaltonRockoff200935-93] The colonies' distance from Britain facilitated the entrenchment of self-governance,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOtis1764-94] and the [First Great Awakening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Great_Awakening), a series of [Christian revivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_revival), fueled colonial interest in guaranteed [religious liberty](46006.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner19984–5-95]&#x20;

### American Revolution and the early republic (1765–1800)

Main articles: [History of the United States (1776–1789)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1776%E2%80%931789\)), [History of the United States (1789–1815)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1789%E2%80%931815\)), [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution), and [Founding Fathers of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States)

See caption.

The *[Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_\(painting\))* portrait depicts the [Committee of Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Five) presenting [the Declaration](31874.md) to the [Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress) on June 28, 1776, in [Philadelphia](50585.md).

Following its victory in the [French and Indian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_and_Indian_War), Britain began to assert greater control over local affairs in the Thirteen Colonies, resulting in [growing political resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution). One of the primary grievances of the colonists was the denial of their [rights as Englishmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rights_of_Englishmen), particularly the right to [representation in the British government that taxed them](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_taxation_without_representation). To demonstrate their dissatisfaction and resolve, the [First Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Continental_Congress) met in 1774 and passed the [Continental Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Association), a colonial boycott of British goods enforced by local "[committees of safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_safety_\(American_Revolution\))" that proved effective. The British attempt to then disarm the colonists resulted in the 1775 [Battles of Lexington and Concord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battles_of_Lexington_and_Concord), igniting the [American Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War). At the [Second Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress), the colonies appointed [George Washington Commander-in-Chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_in_the_American_Revolution) of the [Continental Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Army), and created [a committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Five) that named [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson) to draft the [Declaration of Independence](31874.md). Two days after the Second Continental Congress passed the [Lee Resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Resolution) to create an independent, [sovereign](28791.md) nation, the Declaration was adopted on July 4, 1776.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoungNashRaphael20114–7-96] The [political values of the American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Enlightenment) evolved from an armed rebellion demanding reform within an empire to a revolution that created a new social and governing system founded on the defense of [liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty) and the protection of [inalienable natural rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_law); [equality under the law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equality_under_the_law); [sovereignty of the people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_sovereignty);[^cite_note-97] [republicanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicanism_in_the_United_States) over [monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy), [aristocracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristocracy), and other hereditary political power; [civic virtue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civic_virtue); and an intolerance of [political corruption](66220.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuel1972-98] The [Founding Fathers of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States), who included Washington, Jefferson, [John Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams), [Benjamin Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin), [Alexander Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Hamilton), [John Jay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Jay), [James Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison), [Thomas Paine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Paine), and many others, were inspired by [Classical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_republicanism#Classical_antecedents), [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_philosophy), and [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment#Politics) philosophies and ideas.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-SEoP-2006-100]&#x20;

Though in practical effect since its drafting in 1777, the [Articles of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articles_of_Confederation) were ratified in 1781 and formally established a decentralized government that operated until 1789.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYoungNashRaphael20114–7-96] After the British surrender at the [siege of Yorktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Yorktown) in 1781, American sovereignty was internationally recognized by the [Treaty of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1783\)) (1783), through which the U.S. also gained territory stretching west to the Mississippi River, north to present-day Canada, and south to [Spanish Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Florida).[^cite_note-101] The [Northwest Ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Ordinance) (1787) established the precedent by which the country's territory would expand with the [admission of new states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union), rather than the expansion of existing states.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESatō1886352-102]&#x20;

The [U.S. Constitution](31644.md) was drafted at the 1787 [Constitutional Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Convention_\(United_States\)) to overcome certain limitations of the Articles. It went into effect in 1789, creating a [federal republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_republic) governed by [three separate branches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers_under_the_United_States_Constitution) that together formed a system of [checks and balances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Checks_and_balances).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner2020524-103] George Washington [was elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1788%E2%80%9389_United_States_presidential_election) the country's first president under the Constitution, and the [Bill of Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Bill_of_Rights) (a series of ten amendments to the Constitution) was adopted in 1791 to allay skeptics' concerns about the power of the more centralized government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner2020538–540-104] [Washington's resignation as Commander-in-Chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington%27s_resignation_as_commander-in-chief) after the Revolutionary War and his later refusal to run for a third term as the country's first president established a precedent for the [supremacy of civil authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_control_of_the_military) in the United States and the [peaceful transfer of power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peaceful_transition_of_power).[^cite_note-BoyerJr.20072-105]&#x20;

### Westward expansion and Civil War (1800–1865)

Main articles: [History of the United States (1815–1849)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1815%E2%80%931849\)) and [History of the United States (1849–1865)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1849%E2%80%931865\))

Historical territorial expansion of the United States

[Territorial expansion of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_evolution_of_the_United_States)

In the late 18th century, American settlers began to [expand westward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_evolution_of_the_United_States) in larger numbers, many with a sense of [manifest destiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manifest_destiny).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarlisleGolson2007238-106][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcPherson198841–46-107] The [Louisiana Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Purchase) of 1803 from France nearly doubled the territory of the United States.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] [Lingering issues with Britain remained](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origins_of_the_War_of_1812), leading to the [War of 1812](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_1812), which was fought to a draw.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWait199978-110] [Spain ceded Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adams%E2%80%93On%C3%ADs_Treaty) and its Gulf Coast territory in 1819.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKloseJones1994150-111]&#x20;

The [Missouri Compromise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Compromise) of 1820, which admitted [Missouri](19571.md) as a [slave state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_states_and_free_states) and [Maine](19977.md#Statehood) as a free state, attempted to balance the desire of northern states to prevent the expansion of slavery into new territories with that of southern states to extend it there. Primarily, the compromise prohibited slavery in all other lands of the Louisiana Purchase north of the [36°30′ parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_36%C2%B030%E2%80%B2_north).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHammond2019-112]&#x20;

As Americans expanded further into territory inhabited by Native Americans, the [federal government implemented policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Indian_Policy) of [Indian removal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_removal) or [assimilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_assimilation_of_Native_Americans).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrymer2017-113][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECalloway2019-114] The most significant such legislation was the [Indian Removal Act of 1830](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Removal_Act), a key policy of President [Andrew Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Jackson). It resulted in the [Trail of Tears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail_of_Tears) (1830–1850), in which an estimated 60,000 Native Americans living east of the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River) were forcibly removed and displaced to lands far to the west, causing 13,200 to 16,700 deaths along the [forced march](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_march).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcPherson198845-115] Settler expansion as well as this influx of Indigenous peoples from the East resulted in the [American Indian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Indian_Wars) west of the Mississippi.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichno2003-116][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBillingtonRidge200122-117]&#x20;

During the colonial period, [slavery became legal in all the Thirteen colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_colonial_history_of_the_United_States), and by 1770 it provided the main labor force in the [large-scale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plantation_complexes_in_the_Southern_United_States), [agriculture-dependent economies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antebellum_South#Economic_structure) of the [Southern Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Colonies) from Maryland to Georgia. The practice began to be significantly questioned during the American Revolution,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalker_Howe200752–54Wright2022-118] and spurred by an active [abolitionist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolitionism_in_the_United_States) that had reemerged in the 1830s, states in [the North](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_United_States) enacted laws to prohibit slavery within their boundaries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalker_Howe200752–54Rodriguez2015XXXIVWright2022-119] At the same time, [support for slavery had strengthened in Southern states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-slavery_ideology_in_the_United_States), with widespread use of inventions such as the [cotton gin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton_gin) (1793) having made slavery immensely profitable for [Southern elites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planter_class).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaltonRockoff200943-120][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordon200427–29-121][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalker_Howe2007478,_481–482,_587–588-122]&#x20;

The United States [annexed the Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_annexation) in 1845,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorrison199713–21-123] and the 1846 [Oregon Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oregon_Treaty) led to U.S. control of the present-day [American Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKemp2010180-124] Dispute with Mexico over Texas led to the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War) (1846–1848). After the victory of the U.S., Mexico recognized U.S. sovereignty over Texas, [New Mexico](21649.md), and [California](5407.md) in the 1848 [Mexican Cession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Cession); the cession's lands also included the future states of [Nevada](21216.md), [Colorado](5399.md) and [Utah](31716.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarlisleGolson2007238-106][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcIlwraithMuller200161-125] The [California gold rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_gold_rush) of 1848–1849 spurred a huge migration of white settlers to the Pacific coast, leading to even more confrontations with Native populations. One of the most violent, the [California genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_genocide) of thousands of Native inhabitants, lasted into the mid-1870s.[^cite_note-126] Additional western territories and states were created.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERawls199920-127]&#x20;



[Slave states and free states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_states_and_free_states) in 1858

Throughout the 1850s, the [sectional conflict regarding slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_states_and_free_states) was further inflamed by national legislation in the U.S. Congress and decisions of the Supreme Court. In Congress, the [Fugitive Slave Act of 1850](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fugitive_Slave_Act_of_1850) mandated the forcible return to their enslavers in the South of persons taking refuge in non-slave states, while the [Kansas–Nebraska Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas%E2%80%93Nebraska_Act) of 1854 effectively gutted the anti-slavery requirements of the Missouri Compromise.[^cite_note-128] In its [Dred Scott decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dred_Scott_v._Sandford) of 1857, the Supreme Court ruled against an enslaved person brought into non-slave territory, simultaneously declaring the entire Missouri Compromise to be unconstitutional. These and other [events exacerbated tensions between North and South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origins_of_the_American_Civil_War) that would culminate in the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) (1861–1865).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMurray200476-129][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis200153-130][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoods2012-131]&#x20;

[Beginning with South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Carolina_in_the_American_Civil_War), 11 slave-state governments voted to [secede from the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secession_in_the_United_States) in 1860–1861, joining to create the [Confederate States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America).[^cite_note-Review-Secession-132] All other state governments remained loyal to the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\)).[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESilkenat201925-134][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcPherson1988236-135] War broke out in April 1861 after the Confederacy [bombarded Fort Sumter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Fort_Sumter).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVinovskis19904-136][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcPherson1988273–274-137] Following the [Emancipation Proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emancipation_Proclamation) on January 1, 1863, many freed slaves joined the [Union army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_army).[^cite_note-138] The war [began to turn in the Union's favor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turning_point_of_the_American_Civil_War) following the 1863 [Siege of Vicksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Vicksburg) and [Battle of Gettysburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Gettysburg), and the Confederates surrendered in 1865 after the Union's victory in the [Battle of Appomattox Court House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Appomattox_Court_House).[^cite_note-139]&#x20;

### Reconstruction, Gilded Age, and Progressive Era (1866–1917)

Main article: [History of the United States (1865–1917)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1865%E2%80%931917\))

An [Edison Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edison_Studios) film showing [immigrants](15051.md) arriving at [Ellis Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellis_Island) in [New York Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Harbor), a major point of entry for European immigrants in the late 19th and early 20th centuries

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPriceBenton-Short2008\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksid_Tb5HMB63xACpgPA51_51\]-140]

[^cite_note-141]

Efforts toward [reconstruction in the secessionist South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_era) had begun as early as 1862,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDubois1935163-142] but it was only after [President Lincoln's assassination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Abraham_Lincoln) that the three [Reconstruction Amendments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_Amendments) to the Constitution were ratified [to protect civil rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_rights_movement_\(1865%E2%80%931896\)). The amendments codified nationally the abolition of slavery and involuntary servitude except as punishment for crimes, promised equal protection under the law for all persons, and prohibited denial or abridgment of voting rights on the basis of race, color, or previous enslavement.[^cite_note-Review-Fifteenth-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner198866–7,_251–261,_446–449-145][^cite_note-146] As a result, African Americans took an active political role in ex-Confederate states in the decade following the Civil War.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner1988xxv-147][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDubois1935381–727-148] The former Confederate states were readmitted to the Union, beginning with Tennessee in 1866 and ending with Georgia in 1870.[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150]&#x20;

National infrastructure, including [transcontinental telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_transcontinental_telegraph) and [railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_transcontinental_railroad), spurred growth in the [American frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_frontier). This was accelerated by the [Homestead Acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homestead_Acts), through which nearly 10 percent of the total land area of the United States was given away free to some 1.6 million homesteaders.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlack2011275-151][^cite_note-152] From 1865 through 1917, an unprecedented stream of [immigrants](4599312.md) arrived in the United States, including 24.4 million from Europe.[^cite_note-153] Most came through the [Port of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey), as New York City and other major cities on the [East Coast](89126.md) became home to large [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_the_United_States), [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Americans), and [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Americans) populations. Many [Northern Europeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Europe) as well as significant numbers of [Germans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans) and other [Central Europeans](5188.md) moved to the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States). At the same time, about one million [French Canadians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French-Canadian_Americans) migrated from [Quebec](7954867.md) to [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England).[^cite_note-154] During the [Great Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Migration_\(African_American\)), millions of African Americans [left the rural South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_economy) for urban areas in the North.[^cite_note-155] [Alaska was purchased](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Purchase) from [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire) in 1867.[^cite_note-156]&#x20;

The [Compromise of 1877](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compromise_of_1877) is generally considered the end of the Reconstruction era, as it resolved the electoral crisis following the [1876 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1876_United_States_presidential_election) and led President [Rutherford B. Hayes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutherford_B._Hayes) to reduce the role of federal troops in the South.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFoner1988xxvii,_575–587-157] Immediately, the [Redeemers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redeemers) began evicting the [Carpetbaggers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpetbaggers) and quickly regained local control of Southern politics in the name of [white supremacy](33178.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodward1991-158][^cite_note-159] African Americans endured a period of heightened, overt racism following Reconstruction, a time often considered the [nadir of American race relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadir_of_American_race_relations).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETrelease1979-160][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowman1993\[httpsarchiveorgdetailsmasterslordsmid10000bowmpage221_221\]-161] A series of Supreme Court decisions, including *[Plessy v. Ferguson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plessy_v._Ferguson)*, emptied the [Fourteenth](31666.md) and [Fifteenth Amendments](31667.md) of their force, allowing [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws) in the South to remain unchecked, [sundown towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sundown_town) in the Midwest, and [segregation in communities across the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation_in_the_United_States), which would be reinforced in part by the policy of [redlining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redlining) later adopted by the federal [Home Owners' Loan Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_Owners%27_Loan_Corporation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWare2021-162]&#x20;

[An explosion of technological advancement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Industrial_Revolution), accompanied by the [exploitation of cheap immigrant labor](40067623.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHirschmanMogford2009-163] led to rapid economic expansion during the [Gilded Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilded_Age) of the late 19th century. It continued into the early 20th, when the United States already outpaced the economies of Britain, France, and Germany combined. [Tycoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tycoon) led the nation's expansion in the [railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_rail_transportation_in_the_United_States), [petroleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_petroleum_industry_in_the_United_States), and [steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_steel_industry_\(1850%E2%80%931970\)) industries, as the United States emerged as a pioneer of the [automotive industry](23576520.md).[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarsonBonk1999-165][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERiggs20151179-166][^cite_note-167] This fostered the amassing of enormous economic and political power by [a few prominent industrialists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robber_baron_\(industrialist\)), largely through the formation of [trusts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trust_\(business\)) and [monopolies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monopoly) to prevent competition.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDole1907-168] This resulted in significant increases in [economic inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_inequality), [slum conditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/How_the_Other_Half_Lives), and [civil unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_incidents_of_civil_unrest_in_the_United_States), creating a fertile environment for [labor unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_history_of_the_United_States) to flourish and, to a more limited extent, [socialist movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_in_the_United_States) to emerge.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZinn2005321–357-170][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFraser201566-171] This period eventually ended with the advent of the [Progressive Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Era), which was characterized by significant economic, legislative and social reforms.[^cite_note-Aldrich2-172][^cite_note-173]&#x20;

Pro-American elements in Hawaii [overthrew the Hawaiian monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overthrow_of_the_Hawaiian_Kingdom); the islands [were annexed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Resolution) in 1898. That same year, [Puerto Rico](23041.md), [the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Philippines), and [Guam](11974.md) were ceded to the U.S. by Spain after the latter's defeat in the [Spanish–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish%E2%80%93American_War). (The Philippines was granted full independence from the U.S. on July 4, 1946, following World War II. Puerto Rico and Guam have remained U.S. territories.)[^cite_note-174] [American Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Samoa) was acquired by the United States in 1900 after the [Second Samoan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Samoan_Civil_War).[^cite_note-175] The [U.S. Virgin Islands](32135.md) were purchased from [Denmark](76972.md) in 1917 after Danish voters approved the sale in [a 1916 referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1916_Danish_West_Indies_status_referendum).[^cite_note-176]&#x20;

### World War I, Great Depression, and World War II (1917–1945)

Main article: [History of the United States (1917–1945)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1917%E2%80%931945\))



U.S. marines raise the American flag atop [Mount Suribachi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Suribachi) during the [Battle of Iwo Jima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Iwo_Jima) (1945). This photo became one of the most honored images of [World War II](32927.md).

The United States [entered World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_entry_into_World_War_I) alongside the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_I) in 1917 helping to turn the tide against the [Central Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Powers).[^cite_note-177] In 1920, [a constitutional amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nineteenth_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution) granted nationwide [women's suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_suffrage_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELarsonMeltvedt2021-178] During the 1920s and 1930s, radio for [mass communication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_communication) and early television transformed communications nationwide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinchester2013410–411-179] The [Wall Street Crash of 1929](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_Crash_of_1929) had triggered the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression_in_the_United_States), to which President [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt) responded with the [New Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Deal) plan of "reform, recovery and relief", a series of unprecedented and sweeping [recovery programs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alphabet_agencies) and [employment relief projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Works_Progress_Administration) combined with [financial reforms and regulations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regulatory_economics).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAxinnStern2007-180][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGregory1991-181] During the 1930s, [severe drought and dust storms caused or exacerbated by poor agricultural practices](59749.md) forced hundreds of thousands of displaced farming families (often called ["Okies"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okie) because many originated from [Oklahoma](22489.md)) to migrate from the [Great Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plains) to California in search of subsistence.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStein1973-184]&#x20;

[Initially neutral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_non-interventionism_before_entering_World_War_II) during [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_United_States_during_World_War_II), the U.S. began [supplying war materiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lend-Lease) to the [Allies of World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II) in March 1941 and [entered the war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_entry_into_World_War_II) in December after [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan)'s [attack on Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor).[^cite_note-Pearl_Harbor2-185] Agreeing to a "[Europe first](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_first)" policy, the U.S. concentrated its wartime efforts on Japan's allies [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist_Italy) and [Germany](21212.md) until their final defeat in May 1945. The U.S. [developed the first nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Project) and [used them against the Japanese cities of Hiroshima and Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki) in August 1945, which [historians consider central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debate_over_the_atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki) to the [end of World War II in Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_World_War_II_in_Asia).[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] The United States was one of the "[Four Policemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Policemen)" who met to plan the [post-war world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aftermath_of_World_War_II), alongside the [United Kingdom](31717.md), the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_\(1912%E2%80%931949\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoopesBrinkley1997100-188][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGaddis197225-189] The U.S. emerged relatively unscathed from the war, with even greater [economic power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_power) and [international political influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sphere_of_influence).[^cite_note-190]&#x20;

### Cold War and social revolution (1945–1991)

Main articles: [History of the United States (1945–1964)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1945%E2%80%931964\)), [History of the United States (1964–1980)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1964%E2%80%931980\)), and [History of the United States (1980–1991)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1980%E2%80%931991\))



Civil rights activists during the [March on Washington for Jobs and Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_on_Washington_for_Jobs_and_Freedom) in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) in August 1963

The end of [World War II](32927.md) in 1945 left the U.S. and the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) as [superpowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower), each with its own political, military, and economic [sphere of influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sphere_of_influence). Geopolitical tensions between the two superpowers soon led to the [Cold War](325329.md).[^cite_note-Blakemore-20192-191][^cite_note-192][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESempa2017-193] The Soviets' development of their own atomic weapons led to a fierce [nuclear arms race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_arms_race) with America. The U.S. implemented a policy of [containment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Containment) intended to limit the Soviet Union's sphere of influence; engaged in [regime change against governments perceived to be aligned with the Soviets](37258993.md#1945%E2%80%931991:_Cold_War); and prevailed in the [Space Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Race), which culminated with the [first crewed Moon landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11) in 1969.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlakeley2009\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidrft8AgAAQBAJlpgPA92pgPA85vonepageqffalse_85-96\]-194][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECollins1988-195]&#x20;

Domestically, the U.S. [experienced drastic economic growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post%E2%80%93World_War_II_economic_expansion), [urbanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanization_in_the_United_States), and [population growth following World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-20th_century_baby_boom).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinchester2013305–308-196] The [civil rights movement](49001.md) emerged, with [Martin Luther King Jr.](20076.md) becoming a prominent leader in the early 1960s.[^cite_note-197] The [Great Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Society) plan of President [Lyndon B. Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_B._Johnson)'s administration resulted in groundbreaking and broad-reaching laws, policies and [a constitutional amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-fourth_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution) to counteract some of the worst effects of lingering [institutional racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institutional_racism).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrinkley1991472-198]&#x20;

The [counterculture movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterculture_of_the_1960s) in the U.S. brought significant social changes, including the liberalization of attitudes toward [recreational drug use](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recreational_drug_use) and [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_revolution).[^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] It also encouraged [open defiance of the military draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Draft_evasion_in_the_Vietnam_War) (leading to the [end of conscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_the_United_States) in 1973)[^cite_note-201] and [wide opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_to_United_States_involvement_in_the_Vietnam_War) to [U.S. intervention in Vietnam](8453309.md), with the U.S. totally withdrawing in 1975.[^cite_note-202] [A societal shift in the roles of women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_liberation_movement_in_the_United_States) was significantly responsible for the large increase in female paid labor participation starting in the 1970s, and by 1985 the majority of American women aged 16 and older were employed.[^cite_note-203]&#x20;

The [Fall of Communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989) and the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) from 1989 to 1991 marked the [end of the Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End_of_the_Cold_War) and [left the United States as the world's sole superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower#Post%E2%80%93Cold_War_era).[^cite_note-204] This cemented the United States' global influence, reinforcing the concept of the "[American Century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Century)" as the U.S. dominated international political, cultural, economic, and military affairs.[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206]&#x20;

### Contemporary (1991–present)

Main articles: [History of the United States (1991–2016)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(1991%E2%80%932016\)) and [History of the United States (2016–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_United_States_\(2016%E2%80%93present\))



The [Twin Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) in [Lower Manhattan](455646.md) during the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) of 2001

The 1990s saw the [longest recorded economic expansion in American history up to that time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990s_United_States_boom),[^cite_note-Review-NBER2020-207] a dramatic [decline in U.S. crime rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_the_United_States#Crime_over_time), and [advances in technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technological_and_industrial_history_of_the_United_States#Computers_and_information_networks). Throughout this decade, technological innovations such as the [World Wide Web](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Web), the evolution of the [Pentium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentium_\(original\)) microprocessor in accordance with [Moore's law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moore%27s_law), rechargeable [lithium-ion batteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium-ion_battery), the first [gene therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_therapy) trial, and [cloning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloning) either emerged in the U.S. or were improved upon there. In the 1990s, the [Human Genome Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Genome_Project) was launched, while [Nasdaq](21559.md) became the first stock market in the United States to trade online.[^cite_note-208] In the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) of 1991, [an American-led international coalition of states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_Gulf_War) expelled an [Iraqi](26215470.md) invasion force that had occupied neighboring [Kuwait](7515890.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolsti201120-209] The [September 11 attacks](5058690.md) on the United States in 2001 by the [pan-Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Islamism) militant organization [al-Qaeda](1921.md) led to the U.S. launching the [war on terror](13425800.md) and military interventions [in Afghanistan](19666611.md) and [Iraq](5043324.md).[^cite_note-210] The [U.S. housing bubble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000s_United_States_housing_bubble) culminated in 2007 with the [Great Recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Recession), the largest economic contraction since the Great Depression.[^cite_note-211]&#x20;

In the 2020s, academics and commentators have noted China's economic rise and its growing challenge to the U.S. as the world's sole superpower.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213][^cite_note-x582-214]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_the_United_States)



A [topographic map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topographic_map) of the United States

Although the United States is often cited as the third-largest country by total area (behind Russia and Canada),[^cite_note-largestcountry-21] Wikipedia ranks it [fourth-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area) (after Russia, Canada, and China) because coastal and territorial waters are excluded from each country.[^cite_note-CensusGov2010HTML-18][^cite_note-215] In 2021, the United States had 8% of the Earth's permanent meadows and pastures and 10% of its cropland.[^cite_note-Food_and_Agriculture_Organization_of_the_United_Nations-2023-216]&#x20;

Starting in the east, [the coastal plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Plain) of the [Atlantic](698.md) seaboard gives way to inland forests and rolling hills in the [Piedmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piedmont_\(United_States\)) plateau region.[^cite_note-217] The [Appalachian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_Mountains) and the [Adirondack Massif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adirondack_Mountains) separate the [East Coast](89126.md) from the [Great Lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes) and the grasslands of [the Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States).[^cite_note-NAU-218] The [Mississippi River System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River_System), the world's [fourth-longest river system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_by_length), runs predominantly north–south through the center of the country. The flat and fertile [prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie) of the [Great Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plains) stretches to the west, interrupted by [a highland region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Interior_Highlands) in the southeast.[^cite_note-NAU-218]&#x20;



The [Grand Canyon](46989.md) in Arizona

The [Rocky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountains), west of the Great Plains, extend north to south across the country, peaking at over 14,000 feet (4,300 m) in [Colorado](5399.md).[^cite_note-219] The [supervolcano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supervolcano) underlying [Yellowstone National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowstone_National_Park) in the Rocky Mountains, the [Yellowstone Caldera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowstone_Caldera), is the continent's largest volcanic feature.[^cite_note-220] Farther west are the rocky [Great Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Basin) and the [Chihuahuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chihuahuan_Desert), [Sonoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonoran_Desert), and [Mojave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mojave_Desert) deserts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETinkham1944-221] In the northwest corner of [Arizona](21883824.md), carved by the [Colorado River](180968.md), is the [Grand Canyon](46989.md), a steep-sided canyon and popular tourist destination[^cite_note-222] known for its overwhelming visual size and intricate, colorful landscape. The [Cascade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cascade_Range) and [Sierra Nevada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Nevada) mountain ranges run close to the [Pacific coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_of_the_United_States). The [lowest and highest points in the contiguous United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_points_of_the_United_States) are in the state of California,[^cite_note-223] about 84 miles (135 km) apart.[^cite_note-224]&#x20;

[Alaska](624.md) is a state in the northwestern regions of [North America](21139.md). It is a [semi-exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-exclave) and separated from the [contiguous United States](83759.md). At an elevation of 20,310 feet (6,190.5 m), Alaska's [Denali](207247.md) (also called Mount McKinley) is the highest peak in the country and on the continent.[^cite_note-225] Active [volcanoes in the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_volcanoes_in_the_United_States) are common throughout Alaska's [Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Archipelago) and [Aleutian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands), while [Hawaii has six active volcanoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_volcanoes_in_the_Hawaiian%E2%80%93Emperor_seamount_chain), including [Kilauea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilauea) and [Mauna Loa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauna_Loa).[^cite_note-Review-HawaiiVolcanoes-226] Located entirely outside North America, the Hawaiian archipelago consists of volcanic islands, all [physiographically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physical_geography) and [ethnologically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnology) part of the [Polynesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesia) subregion of [Oceania](22621.md).[^cite_note-227]&#x20;

In addition to its total land area, the United States has one of the world's largest marine [exclusive economic zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zones), spanning approximately 4.5 million square miles (11.7 million km<sup>2</sup>) of ocean.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]&#x20;

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_the_United_States)

See also: [Climate change in the United States](18246585.md)



The [Köppen climate types](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) of the United States

With its large size and geographic variety, the United States includes most climate types. East of the [100th meridian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100th_meridian_west), the climate ranges from [humid continental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) in the north to [humid subtropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) in the south.[^cite_note-230] The western Great Plains are [semi-arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-arid_climate).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGranahanWonkka202432-231] Many mountainous areas of the American West have an [alpine climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_climate). The climate is [arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert_climate) in the Southwest, [Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate) in [coastal California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_California), and [oceanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate) in coastal [Oregon](26811621.md), [Washington](13015878.md), and southern [Alaska](624.md). Most of Alaska is [subarctic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subarctic_climate) or [polar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polar_climate). [Hawaii](13270.md), the [southern tip of Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Florida) and U.S. territories in the [Caribbean](18956035.md) and [Pacific](23070.md) are [tropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_climate).[^cite_note-232]&#x20;

The United States receives more high-impact [extreme weather](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_weather) incidents than any other country.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] States bordering the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md) are prone to hurricanes, and most of the world's tornadoes [occur in the country](18436515.md), mainly in [Tornado Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornado_Alley).[^cite_note-235] Due to [climate change](5042951.md), extreme weather has become more frequent in the U.S. in the 21st century, with three times the number of reported [heat waves](293270.md) compared to the 1960s.[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] Since the 1990s, droughts in the [American Southwest](179571.md) have become more persistent and more severe.[^cite_note-239] The regions considered as the most attractive to the population are the most vulnerable.[^cite_note-240]&#x20;

### Biodiversity and conservation

Main articles: [Fauna of the United States](8853256.md) and [Flora of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_the_United_States)

A bald eagle

The [bald eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bald_eagle) has been the national emblem of the United States since 1782, and was officially declared the national bird in 2024.

[^cite_note-241]

The U.S. is one of 17 [megadiverse countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries) containing large numbers of [endemic species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_endangered_animals_of_North_America): about 17,000 species of [vascular plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vascular_plant) occur in the contiguous United States and Alaska, and over 1,800 species of [flowering plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flowering_plant) are found in Hawaii, few of which occur on the mainland.[^cite_note-242] The United States is home to 428 [mammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mammal) species, 784 birds, 311 reptiles, 295 [amphibians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibian),[^cite_note-Current_Results_#_of_native_species_in_the_US-243] and around 91,000 insect species.[^cite_note-244]&#x20;

There are [63 national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_the_United_States), and [hundreds of other federally managed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_lands) monuments, forests, and [wilderness areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Wilderness_Preservation_System), administered by the [National Park Service](161535.md) and other agencies.[^cite_note-245] About 28% of the country's land is publicly owned and federally managed,[^cite_note-NYTimes_Federal_Land-246] primarily in the [Western States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States).[^cite_note-AKLeg_CRS_Federal_Land-247] [Most of this land is protected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_the_United_States), though some is leased for commercial use, and less than one percent is used for military purposes.[^cite_note-Federal_Land_Ownership-248][^cite_note-Fed_Land_Uses-249]&#x20;

[Environmental issues in the United States](15990862.md) include debates on [non-renewable resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-renewable_resource) and [nuclear energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_debate), [air and water pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollution_prevention_in_the_US), [biodiversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity), logging and [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_in_the_United_States),[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] and [climate change](18246585.md).[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253] The [U.S. Environmental Protection Agency](58666.md) (EPA) is the federal agency charged with [addressing most environmental-related issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_policy_of_the_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECollin2006\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidOVPoqXeTYTwCpgPA1_1\]-254] The [idea of wilderness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Wilderness_Preservation_System) has shaped the management of public lands since 1964, with the [Wilderness Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilderness_Act).[^cite_note-255] The [Endangered Species Act of 1973](532055.md) provides a way to protect threatened and endangered species and their habitats. The [United States Fish and Wildlife Service](75289.md) implements and enforces the Act.[^cite_note-Office-256] In 2024, the U.S. ranked 35th among 180 countries in the [Environmental Performance Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_Performance_Index).[^cite_note-257]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main article: [Politics of the United States](32021.md)

The Capitol Building

The [Capitol Building](31979.md), seat of legislative government, houses both chambers of [Congress](31756.md).

The White House

The [White House](33057.md), the president's [residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_residence) and workplace, includes offices for [the executive staff](44029.md).

The Supreme Court Building

The [Supreme Court Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Supreme_Court_Building) houses the [nation's highest court](31737.md).

The United States is a federal republic consisting of 50 states and a federal capital district, Washington, D.C. The U.S. asserts sovereignty over five unincorporated territories and several uninhabited island possessions.[^cite_note-HRI-2012-33][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOnuf2010xvii-258] It is the world's oldest surviving federation,[^cite_note-259] and its [presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_system) of federal government has been adopted, in whole or in part, by many newly independent states worldwide following their [decolonization](302084.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERyan2000-260] The Constitution of the United States serves as the country's [supreme legal document](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supremacy_Clause).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurnham200641-261]&#x20;

The United States was the most prominent [liberal democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_democracy) for much of the 20th and early 21st centuries, but has undergone [significant democratic backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding_in_the_United_States); some scholars describe this as a shift toward a [hybrid regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_regime)—a political system combining [autocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) and [democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy) features.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELührmannLindberg20191097-262][^cite_note-Grumbach_2021-263][^cite_note-\:4-264] [Gerrymandering](42223515.md), the manipulation of districts for political advantage, has been the subject of Supreme Court litigation.[^cite_note-Review-Districting-265] Political scientists differ over whether the country remains a democracy experiencing backsliding or has become a [hybrid regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_regime). In September 2025, [Brian Klaas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Klaas) described the United States as a [competitive authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Competitive_authoritarianism) system.[^cite_note-\:6-266] Some scholars have written that the U.S. is drifting toward [oligarchy](22315.md#United_States), where government policy is determined by economic elites and the groups representing them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGottschalk202515,_27,_222,_365,_396–7-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] In a 2026 survey of 119 scholars within the [American Political Science Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Political_Science_Association), most respondents regarded the country as still democratic despite significant backsliding, while a minority considered it no longer democratic.[^cite_note-\:5-271]&#x20;

### Federal government

Main article: [Federal government of the United States](195149.md)

Composed of three branches, all headquartered in Washington, D.C., the federal government is the national government of the United States. The U.S. Constitution establishes a [separation of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers) intended to provide a system of [checks and balances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Checks_and_balances) to prevent any of the three branches from becoming supreme.[^cite_note-272] The three-branch system is known as the [presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_system), in contrast to the [parliamentary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system) where the executive is part of the legislative body. Many countries around the world adopted this aspect of the 1789 Constitution of the United States, especially in the [postcolonial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Postcolonialism) Americas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESundquist1997-273]&#x20;

#### Legislature

The [U.S. Congress](31756.md) is a [bicameral legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral_legislature) made up of the [Senate](24909346.md) and the [House of Representatives](19468510.md). The Senate has 100 members—two residents from each state and elected by that state's voters for a six-year term. The House of Representatives has 435 voting members, elected for a two-year term by the constituency of the [congressional district](449478.md) in which they reside.[^cite_note-Review-HouseQualifications-274] District boundaries are generally drawn by [state legislatures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_legislature_\(United_States\)) or redistricting commissions under state law.[^cite_note-Review-RedistrictingCommissions-275] Every U.S. congressional district must be as nearly equal in population as practicable; each sends one representative to Congress.[^cite_note-Review-Districting-265][^cite_note-276] Election years for senators are staggered so that only one-third of them will be up for election every two years.[^cite_note-277] U.S. representatives are all up for election at the same time every two years.&#x20;

The U.S. Congress makes [federal law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_law), [declares war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_war), has the [power of the purse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_of_the_purse),[^cite_note-278] and has [the power of impeachment](1795376.md).[^cite_note-279] The Senate gives advice and consent to the ratification of treaties by a two-thirds vote of senators present.[^cite_note-Review-SenateTreaties-280] One of the foremost non-legislative functions of Congress is [the power to investigate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_investigation) and oversee the executive branch.[^cite_note-tws2010Sep11t11-281] [Congressional oversight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_oversight) is usually delegated to committees and is facilitated by [Congress' power to issue subpoenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contempt_of_Congress).[^cite_note-tws2010Sep11t13-282] Much of the work of Congress is performed by [a collection of committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_congressional_committee), each appointed for a specific purpose or function. Committee membership is by tradition and statute [bipartisan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bipartisan), but all committees are [chaired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chair_\(officer\)) by a member of the [majority party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_party), who sets the committee agenda.[^cite_note-283]&#x20;

#### Executive

Donald Trump

[Donald Trump](4848272.md)\
[President](24113.md)&#x20;

JD Vance

[JD Vance](53396477.md)\
[Vice President](32759.md)&#x20;

The U.S. president is the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state), [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the military, and [chief executive of the federal government](37570.md). The president appoints the [members of the Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_the_United_States), subject to Senate approval, and names other officials who administer and enforce federal law and policy through [their respective agencies](58232.md).[^cite_note-284] The president has the ability to veto [legislative bills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_\(law\)) from the U.S. Congress before they become law. However, [presidential vetoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veto_power_in_the_United_States) can be overridden by a two-thirds [supermajority](638313.md#Use_in_governments_around_the_world) vote in both chambers of Congress. The president also has clemency power for federal crimes and [can issue pardons](54636577.md). Finally, the president has the authority to issue expansive "[executive orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_orders)" in a number of policy areas, subject to [judicial review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_review_in_the_United_States).&#x20;

Candidates for president campaign with a vice-presidential [running mate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running_mate), and both candidates are elected or defeated together in a presidential election. Unlike other votes in American politics, this is technically an [indirect election](652583.md) in which the winner will be determined by the [U.S. Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College).
